{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_018Zz8gAt51HpaF1McFvSJYK","type":"tool_result","content":"# Flask Foundation\n\n**Type:** web-server\n**Version:** 20260922 V3\n**Tier:** platform\n**Category:** Web Server\n**Foundation:** web-server\n**Description:** Flask application boundary and test client contract\n\nPrerequisites: `stack/common.md`, `languages/python/python.md`\n\n## Foundation Contract\n\n- package: app_server\n- entry: create_app() -> Flask\n- fixture: web_client\n- fixture_source: app_server/testing.py\n\n```python\n# app_server/__init__.py\ndef create_app() -> Flask: ...\n\n# app_server/testing.py\nfrom app_server import create_app\ndef web_client():\n    app = create_app()\n    app.config[\"TESTING\"] = True\n    return app.test_client()\n```\n\n`app_server.testing.web_client()` is a plain callable used directly by programmatic acceptance.\nThe pytest `web_client` fixture delegates to it. The application factory registers the selected\nBlueprint routes, extensions, and error handlers; it does not invent routes or settings.\n\n## Rules\n\n- Group related routes in Flask Blueprints. Keep handlers focused on HTTP input and output; put\n  business behavior in importable services without a Flask dependency.\n- Load configuration through the Python foundation's typed boundary before use. Use the Target's\n  names and defaults. Keep `SECRET_KEY` out of source control and disable debug mode in production.\n- Use the Blueprint's routes, schemas, templates, and error contracts. Return sanitized errors;\n  never expose tracebacks to clients. Preserve Jinja autoescaping.\n- Keep request-bound resources request-scoped. Do not share a SQLite connection or other\n  thread-bound object created in `create_app()` across request threads.\n- Keep startup side effects out of imports and account for the development reloader's extra\n  process. The Python foundation owns launchers, dependencies, and the test command.\n----\n# uv and ruff Best Practices\n\n**Type:** guidance\n**Version:** 20260716 V1  \n**Category:** Technologies\n**Description:** Python toolchain conventions — uv for environments and dependencies, ruff for linting and formatting\n\nTechnology reference for the Python toolchain. Applies to any Python project that selects it. This file does not change between projects.\n\nPrerequisites: `stack/common.md`, `languages/python/python.md`\n\n---\n\n## 1. uv Owns the Environment\n\n**Rule**: uv is the only tool that creates environments, installs packages, and runs project commands. Never use bare `pip`, `python -m venv`, `pipx`, or `poetry` alongside it.\n\n```bash\nuv venv                          # create .venv/ (once per clone)\nuv python pin 3.12               # writes .python-version — committed\nuv add flask python-dotenv       # runtime dep → updates pyproject.toml + uv.lock\nuv add --dev pytest ruff mypy    # dev deps\nuv remove flask                  # removal goes through uv too — never hand-edit installs\nuv sync                          # install exactly what uv.lock says (clone setup)\nuv sync --frozen                 # CI install — fail if lock is stale\nuv run pytest                    # run tools inside the project env without activating\nuv run python app.py\nuvx ruff --version               # one-off tool run, no install into the project\n```\n\nRules:\n- `pyproject.toml` is the manifest; `uv.lock` is committed; `.venv/` and `__pycache__/` are gitignored.\n- Pin the interpreter with `uv python pin` so `.python-version` is committed and every machine resolves the same Python.\n- Prefer `uv run <cmd>` over activating the venv in scripts and CI — it is explicit and cannot pick up the wrong environment.\n- Dependency changes always go through `uv add` / `uv remove` so the lock file never drifts from the manifest.\n- CI uses `uv sync --frozen`; a stale lock fails the build instead of silently re-resolving.\n\n**Why**: One tool with one lock file makes installs deterministic. `works on my machine` is almost always an environment drift problem, and `uv sync --frozen` makes drift a build failure.\n\n---\n\n## 2. ruff Is Linter and Formatter\n\n**Rule**: ruff replaces flake8, isort, pyupgrade, and black. Configure it once in `pyproject.toml`; do not add a second linter or formatter.\n\n```toml\n# pyproject.toml\n[tool.ruff]\nline-length = 88\ntarget-version = \"py312\"\n\n[tool.ruff.lint]\nselect = [\n    \"E\", \"W\",   # pycodestyle\n    \"F\",        # pyflakes\n    \"I\",        # isort — import ordering\n    \"UP\",       # pyupgrade — modern syntax, incl. modern type hints\n    \"B\",        # bugbear — likely bugs\n    \"SIM\",      # simplify\n    \"C4\",       # comprehension cleanups\n]\n\n[tool.ruff.format]\nquote-style = \"double\"\n```\n\n```bash\nuv run ruff check .              # lint\nuv run ruff check . --fix        # apply safe autofixes\nuv run ruff format .             # format\nuv run ruff format . --check     # CI — verify without rewriting\n```\n\nRules:\n- Configuration lives in `pyproject.toml` under `[tool.ruff]` — no `.flake8`, `setup.cfg`, or separate ruff.toml.\n- `target-version` matches `requires-python` so `UP` rules upgrade syntax to what the project can actually use.\n- Suppressions are per-line `# noqa: <RULE>` with a reason; file-wide and rule-wide ignores require justification in the config next to the ignore.\n- Formatting is never debated in review: `ruff format` output is canonical.\n\n**Why**: One fast tool with one config ends linter sprawl. The `UP` ruleset mechanically enforces the modern-syntax rules in `languages/python/python.md` §3.\n\n---\n\n## 3. Gates: Local and CI\n\n**Rule**: Lint, format, and tests run the same way locally and in CI. CI is `uv sync --frozen` followed by the same three commands a developer runs.\n\n```bash\n# bin/check — the single local gate (run before committing)\n#!/usr/bin/env bash\nset -euo pipefail\nuv run ruff check .\nuv run ruff format . --check\nuv run pytest\n```\n\n```yaml\n# CI job steps (see stack/github-actions.md for the full workflow)\n- run: uv sync --frozen\n- run: uv run ruff check .\n- run: uv run ruff format . --check\n- run: uv run pytest\n```\n\nOptional pre-commit hook — keep it to fast, deterministic checks:\n\n```yaml\n# .pre-commit-config.yaml\nrepos:\n  - repo: https://github.com/astral-sh/ruff-pre-commit\n    rev: v0.5.0\n    hooks:\n      - id: ruff\n        args: [--fix]\n      - id: ruff-format\n```\n\n**Why**: When the local gate and CI run identical commands, a green local run predicts a green build. Divergent invocations produce \"passes here, fails there\" churn.\n\n---\n\n## Summary Checklist\n\n- [ ] uv only: `uv venv`, `uv add`/`uv remove`, `uv run` — no bare pip, venv, or second package manager\n- [ ] `pyproject.toml`, `uv.lock`, and `.python-version` committed; `.venv/` gitignored\n- [ ] CI installs with `uv sync --frozen`\n- [ ] ruff configured once in `pyproject.toml` (lint + format); no other linter/formatter\n- [ ] `E/W/F/I/UP/B/SIM/C4` rulesets selected; suppressions are per-line with reasons\n- [ ] Same check commands locally (`bin/check`) and in CI\n----\n# Persistence & Service Encapsulation\n\n**Type:** guidance\n**Version:** 20260919 V3\n**Category:** Persistence\n**Description:** The single boundary rule for all persistent stores and external services — typed access classes, no raw access in application code\n\nTechnology reference. Framework-agnostic. This file does not change between projects.\n\nPrerequisite: `stack/common.md`\n\n---\n\n## The Invariant\n\n**Rule**: All persistence and external-service access goes through a typed encapsulation\nclass. Route, feature, and business-logic code never executes raw SQL, reads `os.environ`,\nopens application files directly, or imports a cloud SDK (`boto3`). The typed class interface\n— not the underlying schema or service — is the dependency boundary.\n\n**Why**: A storage or service change is absorbed at the class. Downstream code depends on the\ninterface, so a schema change that does not change the interface changes nothing else. This is\nthe same swap-layer discipline a cloud client library enforces for AWS (`import boto3`\noutside the wrapper fails review), generalized to every store.\n\nA code review that finds raw SQL, `os.environ`, `open()` on application data, or a cloud SDK\nimport outside its encapsulation class fails.\n\n**Relational rule**: the relational layer is generated, not written. Drydock emits it from\n`DATABASE.md` and copies the engines in unchanged; the build writes callers. See section 1.\n\n---\n\n## 1. Relational stores → the generated persistence package\n\n**You do not write this layer.** Drydock generates it from `DATABASE.md` at the DATABASE build\nstep and copies the engines in unchanged. Writing a repository, a connection helper, a session\nfactory, or a migration by hand is a review failure: the generated package already implements the\ndeclaration, and a hand-written second path is storage access no Blueprint cites.\n\nThe build writes **callers**.\n\n```text\nroute → service → db.<table>.<verb>(...) → generated package → database\n```\n\nLanguage bindings are shipped in `languages/<language>/persistence/`; generation templates live\nbeside them in `templates/`. Python and Go implement the same declared operations. The language\ninstructions own native names, dependencies and fixtures. Go uses `OpenDatabase(ctx, dsn)` and\ngenerated typed repository methods; the Python examples below use `open_database(dsn)`.\n\nThe Python package lands at `persistence/` in the build:\n\n| File | Origin |\n|---|---|\n| `db.py`, `sqlite.py`, `postgres.py` | shipped by Drydock, copied unchanged |\n| `schema.py` | generated from `DATABASE.md` — tables, operations, row dataclasses |\n| `schema.sql` (beside the package) | generated DDL, applied once at the DATABASE step |\n\n### Calling it\n\n```python\nfrom persistence import open_database\n\ndb = open_database(config.database_url)\n\nticket = db.tickets.get(id=ticket_id)          # returns a frozen dataclass, or None\nopen_tickets = db.tickets.list(status=\"open\")  # returns a list of them\nnew_id = db.tickets.create(status=\"open\", title=title)\ndb.tickets.update(id=ticket_id, status=\"closed\")\ndb.tickets.delete(id=ticket_id)\n```\n\nOne attribute per declared table, one method per declared access pattern. The method name is the\nverb of that pattern's `Repository operation` cell, and its keyword arguments are that pattern's\n`Input:` fields — nothing else is accepted. A verb the table does not declare raises\n`UnknownOperation` naming what it does grant.\n\n**The declaration fixes the surface.** `DATABASE.md` names every operation in the\n`Repository operation` cell of each access pattern, as `<table>.<verb>`. That is the persistence\nsurface, not a suggestion for one. To reach the store differently, change the declaration and\nrebuild; there is no second door.\n\n**Why**: the declaration is the only description of the store the rest of the plan is allowed to\ndepend on. Generating from it means the operations a Blueprint cites and the operations that exist\ncannot drift — they are the same list. Plan already refuses to write a plan whose tables,\noperations, and citations disagree; generating the layer keeps the built code in that agreement\nrather than trusting each build to re-derive it from prose.\n\n**Caller contract**: `Database` presents single-threaded semantics. Callers construct one instance,\nshare it freely, call methods synchronously, and never reason about threads, connections, or\nlocking. Connection lifetime is internal to the package and may change without affecting any\ncaller. Returned rows are frozen dataclasses and carry no connection state.\n\n**Engine selection is runtime, not build time.** Both engines are copied into every build and\nchosen from the DSN, because the same build runs SQLite in development and test and PostgreSQL in\nproduction. `VARCHAR(n)` is declared once and emitted to both: PostgreSQL enforces the length,\nSQLite parses the same declaration and ignores it.\n\n**Schema is built once.** `schema.sql` is applied at the DATABASE build step and never again\nwithin a release. Application startup does not create or alter schema.\n\n**Guardrails**\n\n- Do not write a repository, DAO, ORM model, or session factory.\n- Do not import `sqlite3`, `psycopg`, or a SQL string anywhere outside `persistence/`.\n- Do not edit anything under `persistence/` — edit `DATABASE.md` and rebuild.\n- Do not create or alter schema at application startup.\n\n### Acceptance transactions\n\n**Acceptance store lifecycle.** The DATABASE step provisions and verifies the configured store\nonce. Criteria never replace DATABASE_URL/DATABASE_PATH, create a temporary database, apply DDL,\nor assume an empty store. Database criteria use `with db_session() as db:` (Go:\n`OpenSession(ctx, \"\")` with checked deferred cleanup) and pass that same database into feature\noperations. Setup, behavior and assertions run inside one transaction; the shipped fixture always\nrolls it back. Application operations outside that scope remain autocommitted. Never commit,\nclose the owned connection, or open another connection inside the rollback scope.\n\nHTTP, CLI/subprocess, background-work and commit/restart criteria use committed behavior instead.\nUse `persistence.testing.committed_session()` (Go: `OpenCommittedSession(ctx, \"\")`) for\nsetup/readback/cleanup on the configured store; this helper closes connections, not business records.\nEach owns a stable criterion-specific identifier, reconciles only its own residue before setup,\nasserts setup success, checks only owned records, and uses `try/finally` (Go: checked `defer`) for\ncleanup through supported product interfaces. Cleanup failure fails the criterion; retries must\nwork even when prior cleanup never ran. Immutable ledger records remain identified or receive\nsupported reversal entries; never delete history or reset a shared store. Include independent\ncommit/reconnect durability coverage. Applies to any Python project that selects it. This file does not change between projects.\n\nPrerequisites: `stack/common.md`, `languages/python/python.md`\n\n---\n\n## 1. uv Owns the Environment\n\n**Rule**: uv is the only tool that creates environments, installs packages, and runs project commands. Never use bare `pip`, `python -m venv`, `pipx`, or `poetry` alongside it.\n\n```bash\nuv venv                          # create .venv/ (once per clone)\nuv python pin 3.12               # writes .python-version — committed\nuv add flask python-dotenv       # runtime dep → updates pyproject.toml + uv.lock\nuv add --dev pytest ruff mypy    # dev deps\nuv remove flask                  # removal goes through uv too — never hand-edit installs\nuv sync                          # install exactly what uv.lock says (clone setup)\nuv sync --frozen                 # CI install — fail if lock is stale\nuv run pytest                    # run tools inside the project env without activating\nuv run python app.py\nuvx ruff --version               # one-off tool run, no install into the project\n```\n\nRules:\n- `pyproject.toml` is the manifest; `uv.lock` is committed; `.venv/` and `__pycache__/` are gitignored.\n- Pin the interpreter with `uv python pin` so `.python-version` is committed and every machine resolves the same Python.\n- Prefer `uv run <cmd>` over activating the venv in scripts and CI — it is explicit and cannot pick up the wrong environment.\n- Dependency changes always go through `uv add` / `uv remove` so the lock file never drifts from the manifest.\n- CI uses `uv sync --frozen`; a stale lock fails the build instead of silently re-resolving.\n\n**Why**: One tool with one lock file makes installs deterministic. `works on my machine` is almost always an environment drift problem, and `uv sync --frozen` makes drift a build failure.\n\n---\n\n## 2. ruff Is Linter and Formatter\n\n**Rule**: ruff replaces flake8, isort, pyupgrade, and black. Configure it once in `pyproject.toml`; do not add a second linter or formatter.\n\n```toml\n# pyproject.toml\n[tool.ruff]\nline-length = 88\ntarget-version = \"py312\"\n\n[tool.ruff.lint]\nselect = [\n    \"E\", \"W\",   # pycodestyle\n    \"F\",        # pyflakes\n    \"I\",        # isort — import ordering\n    \"UP\",       # pyupgrade — modern syntax, incl. modern type hints\n    \"B\",        # bugbear — likely bugs\n    \"SIM\",      # simplify\n    \"C4\",       # comprehension cleanups\n]\n\n[tool.ruff.format]\nquote-style = \"double\"\n```\n\n```bash\nuv run ruff check .              # lint\nuv run ruff check . --fix        # apply safe autofixes\nuv run ruff format .             # format\nuv run ruff format . --check     # CI — verify without rewriting\n```\n\nRules:\n- Configuration lives in `pyproject.toml` under `[tool.ruff]` — no `.flake8`, `setup.cfg`, or separate ruff.toml.\n- `target-version` matches `requires-python` so `UP` rules upgrade syntax to what the project can actually use.\n- Suppressions are per-line `# noqa: <RULE>` with a reason; file-wide and rule-wide ignores require justification in the config next to the ignore.\n- Formatting is never debated in review: `ruff format` output is canonical.\n\n**Why**: One fast tool with one config ends linter sprawl. The `UP` ruleset mechanically enforces the modern-syntax rules in `languages/python/python.md` §3.\n\n---\n\n## 3. Gates: Local and CI\n\n**Rule**: Lint, format, and tests run the same way locally and in CI. CI is `uv sync --frozen` followed by the same three commands a developer runs.\n\n```bash\n# bin/check — the single local gate (run before committing)\n#!/usr/bin/env bash\nset -euo pipefail\nuv run ruff check .\nuv run ruff format . --check\nuv run pytest\n```\n\n```yaml\n# CI job steps (see stack/github-actions.md for the full workflow)\n- run: uv sync --frozen\n- run: uv run ruff check .\n- run: uv run ruff format . --check\n- run: uv run pytest\n```\n\nOptional pre-commit hook — keep it to fast, deterministic checks:\n\n```yaml\n# .pre-commit-config.yaml\nrepos:\n  - repo: https://github.com/astral-sh/ruff-pre-commit\n    rev: v0.5.0\n    hooks:\n      - id: ruff\n        args: [--fix]\n      - id: ruff-format\n```\n\n**Why**: When the local gate and CI run identical commands, a green local run predicts a green build. Divergent invocations produce \"passes here, fails there\" churn.\n\n---\n\n## Summary Checklist\n\n- [ ] uv only: `uv venv`, `uv add`/`uv remove`, `uv run` — no bare pip, venv, or second package manager\n- [ ] `pyproject.toml`, `uv.lock`, and `.python-version` committed; `.venv/` gitignored\n- [ ] CI installs with `uv sync --frozen`\n- [ ] ruff configured once in `pyproject.toml` (lint + format); no other linter/formatter\n- [ ] `E/W/F/I/UP/B/SIM/C4` rulesets selected; suppressions are per-line with reasons\n- [ ] Same check commands locally (`bin/check`) and in CI\n----\n# Persistence & Service Encapsulation\n\n**Type:** guidance\n**Version:** 20260919 V3\n**Category:** Persistence\n**Description:** The single boundary rule for all persistent stores and external services — typed access classes, no raw access in application code\n\nTechnology reference. Framework-agnostic. This file does not change between projects.\n\nPrerequisite: `stack/common.md`\n\n---\n\n## The Invariant\n\n**Rule**: All persistence and external-service access goes through a typed encapsulation\nclass. Route, feature, and business-logic code never executes raw SQL, reads `os.environ`,\nopens application files directly, or imports a cloud SDK (`boto3`). The typed class interface\n— not the underlying schema or service — is the dependency boundary.\n\n**Why**: A storage or service change is absorbed at the class. Downstream code depends on the\ninterface, so a schema change that does not change the interface changes nothing else. This is\nthe same swap-layer discipline a cloud client library enforces for AWS (`import boto3`\noutside the wrapper fails review), generalized to every store.\n\nA code review that finds raw SQL, `os.environ`, `open()` on application data, or a cloud SDK\nimport outside its encapsulation class fails.\n\n**Relational rule**: the relational layer is generated, not written. Drydock emits it from\n`DATABASE.md` and copies the engines in unchanged; the build writes callers. See section 1.\n\n---\n\n## 1. Relational stores → the generated persistence package\n\n**You do not write this layer.** Drydock generates it from `DATABASE.md` at the DATABASE build\nstep and copies the engines in unchanged. Writing a repository, a connection helper, a session\nfactory, or a migration by hand is a review failure: the generated package already implements the\ndeclaration, and a hand-written second path is storage access no Blueprint cites.\n\nThe build writes **callers**.\n\n```text\nroute → service → db.<table>.<verb>(...) → generated package → database\n```\n\nLanguage bindings are shipped in `languages/<language>/persistence/`; generation templates live\nbeside them in `templates/`. Python and Go implement the same declared operations. The language\ninstructions own native names, dependencies and fixtures. Go uses `OpenDatabase(ctx, dsn)` and\ngenerated typed repository methods; the Python examples below use `open_database(dsn)`.\n\nThe Python package lands at `persistence/` in the build:\n\n| File | Origin |\n|---|---|\n| `db.py`, `sqlite.py`, `postgres.py` | shipped by Drydock, copied unchanged |\n| `schema.py` | generated from `DATABASE.md` — tables, operations, row dataclasses |\n| `schema.sql` (beside the package) | generated DDL, applied once at the DATABASE step |\n\n### Calling it\n\n```python\nfrom persistence import open_database\n\ndb = open_database(config.database_url)\n\nticket = db.tickets.get(id=ticket_id)          # returns a frozen dataclass, or None\nopen_tickets = db.tickets.list(status=\"open\")  # returns a list of them\nnew_id = db.tickets.create(status=\"open\", title=title)\ndb.tickets.update(id=ticket_id, status=\"closed\")\ndb.tickets.delete(id=ticket_id)\n```\n\nOne attribute per declared table, one method per declared access pattern. The method name is the\nverb of that pattern's `Repository operation` cell, and its keyword arguments are that pattern's\n`Input:` fields — nothing else is accepted. A verb the table does not declare raises\n`UnknownOperation` naming what it does grant.\n\n**The declaration fixes the surface.** `DATABASE.md` names every operation in the\n`Repository operation` cell of each access pattern, as `<table>.<verb>`. That is the persistence\nsurface, not a suggestion for one. To reach the store differently, change the declaration and\nrebuild; there is no second door.\n\n**Why**: the declaration is the only description of the store the rest of the plan is allowed to\ndepend on. Generating from it means the operations a Blueprint cites and the operations that exist\ncannot drift — they are the same list. Plan already refuses to write a plan whose tables,\noperations, and citations disagree; generating the layer keeps the built code in that agreement\nrather than trusting each build to re-derive it from prose.\n\n**Caller contract**: `Database` presents single-threaded semantics. Callers construct one instance,\nshare it freely, call methods synchronously, and never reason about threads, connections, or\nlocking. Connection lifetime is internal to the package and may change without affecting any\ncaller. Returned rows are frozen dataclasses and carry no connection state.\n\n**Engine selection is runtime, not build time.** Both engines are copied into every build and\nchosen from the DSN, because the same build runs SQLite in development and test and PostgreSQL in\nproduction. `VARCHAR(n)` is declared once and emitted to both: PostgreSQL enforces the length,\nSQLite parses the same declaration and ignores it.\n\n**Schema is built once.** `schema.sql` is applied at the DATABASE build step and never again\nwithin a release. Application startup does not create or alter schema.\n\n**Guardrails**\n\n- Do not write a repository, DAO, ORM model, or session factory.\n- Do not import `sqlite3`, `psycopg`, or a SQL string anywhere outside `persistence/`.\n- Do not edit anything under `persistence/` — edit `DATABASE.md` and rebuild.\n- Do not create or alter schema at application startup.\n\n### Acceptance transactions\n\n**Acceptance store lifecycle.** The DATABASE step provisions and verifies the configured store\nonce. Criteria never replace DATABASE_URL/DATABASE_PATH, create a temporary database, apply DDL,\nor assume an empty store. Database criteria use `with db_session() as db:` (Go:\n`OpenSession(ctx, \"\")` with checked deferred cleanup) and pass that same database into feature\noperations. Setup, behavior and assertions run inside one transaction; the shipped fixture always\nrolls it back. Application operations outside that scope remain autocommitted. Never commit,\nclose the owned connection, or open another connection inside the rollback scope.\n\nHTTP, CLI/subprocess, background-work and commit/restart criteria use committed behavior instead.\nUse `persistence.testing.committed_session()` (Go: `OpenCommittedSession(ctx, \"\")`) for\nsetup/readback/cleanup on the configured store; this helper closes connections, not business records.\nEach owns a stable criterion-specific identifier, reconciles only its own residue before setup,\nasserts setup success, checks only owned records, and uses `try/finally` (Go: checked `defer`) for\ncleanup through supported product interfaces. Cleanup failure fails the criterion; retries must\nwork even when prior cleanup never ran. Immutable ledger records remain identified or receive\nsupported reversal entries; never delete history or reset a shared store. Include independent\ncommit/reconnect durability coverage. Database acceptance must not be replaced by a project test\ncount: runtime catalog and row-count evidence proves provisioning, feature criteria prove behavior.\n\n## 2.
